Isn't it like Peirce's agapasm?

If someone else had already discussed the same thing as what I want to call Love, how would it have been written?

In his 1893 paper "Evolutionary Love", Peirce divided the modes of evolution into three: evolution by fortuitous variation, evolution by mechanical necessity, and evolution by creative love (agapasm). Peirce places the first two as degenerate forms of agapasm, and writes that the movement of love is circular, at one and the same impulse projecting creations into independency and drawing them into harmony.

Having seen introductions to that theory through a web search, pjdhiro sees it as follows.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):As I thought, when I look at the introductions to agapasm that come up when I search the web, they express the driving source and development, don't they. It's as if I am reinventing the wheel.

Lining up where the theories are alike and where they differ gives the following.

  • Close: growing not through intention or effort. Peirce writes, "Direct endeavor can achieve almost nothing." pjdhiro sees development as a phenomenon of being developed by the I that has not noticed and by the surrounding environment (What does development point to?).
  • Close: being drawn before it takes form. Peirce writes of an attraction for an idea whose nature is divined before the mind possesses it. pjdhiro puts forward the hypothesis that what drifts as if drawn by the distortions of the field of Love is the universe and us (Isn't Love like gravity?).
  • Close: holding even what is opposed as one aspect. Peirce's love does not have hatred as its contrary. pjdhiro guesses that things like the desire for power and the desire to dominate are also aspects of Love (What is what I want to call Love not?).
  • Different: whether it has a good destination. Peirce's love has the direction of making the hateful lovely. pjdhiro asks, "Isn't it that there is no good and evil, no rationality?" (What is still not known).
  • Different: whether it is discussed as a cosmology. Peirce discusses love as a mode of evolution working in the universe. This reader starts from pjdhiro's experience, and looks at whether its structure is similar to physics and theories.

The key sentences of Peirce's original text and the details of this comparison are on the separate page "Peirce's creative love and abduction" (in Japanese).

How pjdhiro noticed that they may resemble Peirce not only in theory but even in temperament and personality is on the same separate page, in the chapter "Isn't it like Peirce even in temperament?" (in Japanese).

Sources

C. S. Peirce, "Evolutionary Love", The Monist 3(2), 1893, pp.176–200. The three modes of evolution (tychasm, anancasm, agapasm), the sentence that makes the first two degenerate forms of agapasm, and the sentence that the movement of love is circular were checked against the text in the original issue of The Monist vol. 3 no. 2 (Internet Archive) and in the reprint, Chance, Love, and Logic, edited by Cohen (1923, public domain). The passages and pages on direct effort, the attraction before possessing, and the love that does not have hatred as its contrary are on the separate page "Peirce's creative love and abduction" (in Japanese). The page that sets a full Japanese translation beside the original is "Reading Peirce's 'Evolutionary Love' in Japanese" (the translation is by Claude and has not yet been reviewed by a person).

That is the driving source of projects

Here we return to the question posed at the very start of Part 1.

pjdhiro's question (translated by Claude):What drives us to do projects? What drives us to live?

At the head of Part 1, we began from the picture that first there are goals and plans, and people move along them. Going through the parts so far, the answer takes another form.

People move when they feel that something is missing. That missingness is usually called deficiency or needs. As seen in the parts so far, the body receives, as signals, the stability of connection and the gap from it. The gap is felt as a lack, and people are moved by that lack. pjdhiro sees it this way: "I think the words deficiency and needs are also this." The driving source of projects may be this lack. pjdhiro says this lack "means the gap between Love and me". If so, people may be part of the field I want to call Love, and inside its distortion. Not only business but life and family, too, are each a project.

(See also: When signals and thoughts part, one feels a lack, Is being itself the source?)

More — the driving sources of management studies are also vortices in the same distortion

In the table in "What has management studies seen as the driving source?", markets, goals, motivation and the entrepreneur's mind are lined up as driving sources. Following on from the point that there is no framework for analyzing the founders of family businesses that have lasted a thousand years, only about three generations at most, pjdhiro says this.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):So management studies and the concept of management are vortices too

In every project, people move within the distortion of the field without being able to fully intend or fully control.

The table's row for "the inside of the person" was set down as right as long as one does not ask where empathy and motivation well up from. In this view, motivation, and what is called intrinsic motivation too, are vortices in the same distortion, and what they well up from is the lack above ("Intrinsic motivation" in the section on the words of the mind).

After reading this section, pjdhiro added one viewpoint about the entrepreneur's mind.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):There is a viewpoint I want to add here. Aren't the personality and physical traits called entrepreneurial "manifestations of something equivalent"? For example, being driven by Love toward the direction called results is common to humankind, but some people drift in the direction of money, honor or power, and others are drawn to things that don't make money, like knowledge or descendants. I want to treat this phenomenon, which might be called "personality", as equivalent. Not good and bad, but each being a manifestation. Understanding that leads to "acceptance". And of course to the recognition of separation called diversity.

pjdhiro's view of personality as the strength and tendencies of psychological defense is in the chapter "The mind has mechanisms to protect itself".

More — when the lack turns away from creating

The sense of lack does not always head toward creating. Descriptions of personality types offer one example. Riso and Hudson, in their 1996 book on the personality classification called the Enneagram, write that the type that lives toward art and beauty (Type Four) feels that something is missing in itself, yet cannot point to what it is (p.139).

At the average levels, this sense of lack heads not toward creating but toward other ways of filling it. The type tries to fill what is lost inside by intensifying the power that beautiful things have over the feelings (pp.149–150). Much of the creating comes to happen only in the imagination (p.149). When cornered, the type also comes to lean on others, trying to find someone who will love it and so cross the distance between itself and others (pp.163–164).

In the healthy direction, the book writes, the type realizes itself by turning toward something objective beyond feeling and imagination, and what it makes becomes objective (pp.165–167). It also writes that inspiration can be sustained only within the act of creating (pp.144–145).

It can be read that the same lack may drive creating, and may also turn aside into imagination or leaning on others. The dividing line seems to lie in whether the lack is turned toward outward action or filled inside. This reading is Claude's analogy; Riso and Hudson do not write of the lack as a driving source. What kind of classification the Enneagram is is summarized in "What is personality?" (in Japanese).

Sources

Sources: Riso with Hudson (1996) Personality Types: Using the Enneagram for Self-Discovery, revised edition. Houghton Mifflin (chapter 6, Type Four; full text checked in the e-book; free to read through Internet Archive's lending; pages are given by the print-page markers embedded in the e-book; pp.139, 144–145, 149–150, 163–167). Translation by Claude.

Part 5 looked at the word Love and answered the first question. This is the end of the first half of the reader, whose theme was the driving source.

From here is the second half, whose theme is development. Of the title Love-Driven Development, the first half looked at Love and driven. The second half looks at what the word development points to, and heads toward the second question of the opening.

Part 6

What is happening inside what is called development?

What does development point to?

The second question set out at the opening was "Why do we try to become something different from what we were?" Before turning to this question, we look at what the word development points to. pjdhiro questions the commonplace of moving things along as planned like this.

pjdhiro's question (translated by Claude):For example, is the work of business development really something that can be intended, controlled and done consciously?

Seen from the side of research, all three of intention, control and consciousness seem less certain than the words suggest. In the title "Love-Driven Development", the driving source is what I want to call Love, and development is what proceeds driven by it. As seen in the first half, people are inside that field and move without being able to fully intend or fully control. So this page includes in development both what is moved along intentionally and what grows and changes without intention.

The sequence of envisioning, planning and executing is a description that takes out, from what is happening, the parts people can consciously engage with. In the same time that sequence proceeds, processes not written in the sequence also proceed. Among them are things that have not yet happened: what will likely happen, and what may happen. The "development" of the title points to the whole that includes both. The people who think they are moving it are also inside that whole. pjdhiro puts it like this.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):It is the phenomenon of being developed by the me I am not aware of and the environment around me. Though the subject is me.

Let us draw this view as a figure, borrowing the form of analysis that compares the current state (as-is) with the target state (to-be). At every point, the me I think I am at that time is heading for the destination. When one looks back and connects the dots, a path that had been drawn toward what I want to call Love comes into view. The gap does not exist from the start as the distance between now and the destination; it arises between the direction at each moment and the path seen afterward.

More — how certain are intention, control and consciousness?

The word "business development" suggests the act of intentionally starting a business and moving it along as one wishes. Let us look at the three in turn from the side of research.

  • Intention: Mintzberg and Waters (1983) distinguished strategies that were intended and realized, strategies that were intended but not realized, and strategies that took shape without being intended (emergent strategies). Mintzberg also wrote in 1994 that every strategy has some emergent quality.
  • Control: Knight (1921) distinguished "risk", which can be estimated by probability, from "uncertainty", where even the probability is unknown. And Knight thought that business profit arises from taking on this uncertainty.
  • Consciousness: Weick et al. (1999) wrote that people, after starting to act, use the results as clues to find out, looking back, what is happening. pjdhiro sees this as similar to "dots connect looking backward" in Jobs's speech. The paper by Weick and colleagues also introduces a story Weick has cited again and again. A small Hungarian unit lost in a snowstorm in the Alps calms down when one of them finds a map, and relying on the map, makes its way back. When the lieutenant who had sent them out looks at the map, it is a map of the Pyrenees. Weick reads it as saying that when you are lost, any map will do, as long as it gets you moving. However, this story is a poem written in 1977 by the poet Holub from something told by the Nobel laureate Szent-Györgyi, and whether it actually happened has not been confirmed (Basbøll and Graham, 2006). Wegner (1999, 2003) argued that the very feeling that "I did it" may be an interpretation the mind constructs by inferring from the relation between thought and action.

Lined up like this, "business development" may have the aspect of being a name for what happened, arranged afterward into a single story. At the same time, in Knight's distinction, business is also carried forward while taking on a future whose probabilities are unknown. This page asks about what did not make it into that story, and what was moving before the story. The same shape appears within one person, in a group of people, and in society.

Sources: Mintzberg & Waters (1983) "The Mind of the Strategist(s)", in S. Srivastva and Associates, The Executive Mind, Jossey-Bass, pp.58–83 (full text checked against a scan of the book on the author's site; the definitions of the three strategies p.60) / Mintzberg (1994) "Rethinking Strategic Planning Part I", Long Range Planning 27(3):12–21 (full text checked against the PDF of the journal pages on the author's site; the passage on strategies having an emergent quality p.19) / Knight (1921) Risk, Uncertainty and Profit (full text checked against the public-domain full text in Liberty Fund's Library of Economics and Liberty; the distinction between risk and uncertainty in paragraph III.VIII.1 of Part III chapter 8, profit and uncertainty in paragraph III.X.33 of Part III chapter 10) / Colville, Waterman & Weick (1999) "Organizing and the Search for Excellence: Making Sense of the Times in Theory and Practice", Organization 6(1):129–148 (full text checked against the Wayback Machine record of the PDF of the journal pages that was in the University of Michigan institutional repository (20 August 2017); the passage citing p.55 of Weick 1995 p.141; the story of the unit and the map pp.141–142, citing p.54 of Weick 1995; the reading that any map will do p.142) / Basbøll & Graham (2006) "Substitutes for Strategy Research: Notes on the source of Karl Weick's anecdote of the young lieutenant and the map of the Pyrenees", ephemera 6(2):194–204 (full text checked against the PDF published by the journal; that the story comes from Szent-Györgyi's telling and Holub's 1977 poem, and that it has never been confirmed as historical fact, p.196) / Wegner & Wheatley (1999) "Apparent mental causation", American Psychologist 54(7):480 (full text checked against the Wayback Machine record of the PDF of the journal pages that was on the author's Harvard University page (15 December 2005); abstract p.480) / Wegner (2003) "The mind's best trick", Trends in Cognitive Sciences 7(2):65 (full text checked against the Wayback Machine record of the PDF of the journal pages that was on the same page (9 November 2005); p.65).

More — "development" in the dictionary, and "development" on this page

In Japanese dictionaries, kaihatsu (development) means clearing forest or wasteland to make fields, making use of natural resources to start industries, devising new technologies or products and putting them to practical use, and drawing out wisdom or abilities. In every sense, it is written as an act in which a person stands as the subject and intentionally changes land, resources or products.

Older usages keep a different direction too. Before the Meiji era it was often read kaihotsu, and the Shōbōgenzō has the usage of a practitioner who "was opened up (kaihotsu) by the cook and had a great awakening". It is a usage in which the person stands on the side of being opened and guided. An encyclopedia also writes that English develop means development as a transitive verb and growth as an intransitive verb.

The "development" of this page places the driving source in what I want to call Love, and places people inside that field. Moving things along intentionally is included both in the dictionary's "development" and in this page's. The difference is whether one sees the part moved along intentionally as the whole of development, or as a part people have been able to take out of the whole.

Sources: Seisenban Nihon kokugo daijiten, "開発 (kaihatsu)" senses 1–6 and supplementary note, and the usage example of "開発 (kaihotsu)" (Shōbōgenzō) / Dejitaru Daijisen, "開発" senses 1–3 and supplementary explanation / Nihon daihyakka zensho (Nipponica), "開発", the passage on develop (all checked against the text on Kotobank, 2026-11-10; in Japanese).

The breadth of development as this page uses it is laid out in six: who, what, when, where, why and how.

Question Development as this page uses it
What What is moved along intentionally. What grows and changes without intention
Who One person, a group of people, or society. Even the people making something sometimes cannot tell whether they are making it or it is arising from what surrounds them
When Neither its beginning nor its end is settled as one. Since before the vision is put into words. Beneath envisioning, planning and executing while they go on. Also toward what will likely happen and what may happen
Where Not limited to business. In the projects of life and family too
Why The question is the driving source. The first half answered that it may be what I want to call Love (That is the driving source of projects)
How It proceeds by procedures that can be written in words and numbers. It also proceeds by what one does unconsciously, what is tacit, what is irrational, by feelings and motivation, and by readiness of mind. Force majeure and events not well understood also pile up. It also proceeds by dealing with what cannot be known ahead (dealing with uncertainty)
More — what grows without intention is also included in development

The word development is easily taken, as in business development or product development, as the act of people intentionally making something and moving it along as they wish. Moving things along intentionally certainly is part of development. But as seen at the head of this chapter, intention, control and consciousness all seem less certain than the words suggest. pjdhiro includes in development what is developed unconsciously without intention, what grows, and what changes.

After reading this section, pjdhiro posed this question.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):To begin with, have we been able to grow intentionally? All the time since we were born. Even as adults, we learn from raising children, learn from customers' complaints, and keep growing without intending to, and then age without wanting to.

The body receives signals, feelings move, relationships between people change, and the field fluctuates. pjdhiro sees force majeure and events not well understood piling up there too. In the fold "A structure, a viewpoint, a principle, or a process?" in the chapter "What does Love-Driven Development see as the driving source?", it was written that something invisible keeps moving beneath the vision even after it is put into words.

More — when does development begin, and when does it end?

The sequence of envisioning, planning and executing can be given a beginning and an end. Setting deadlines and drawing divisions lets one check progress. But those divisions are cut out by people from what is happening and attached to it. Processes not written in the sequence begin before the divisions and continue after them.

The beginning is in most cases seen afterward. As seen in the chapter "What drives a project?", dots may connect looking back, as if preparation within one person had already begun. The field may fluctuate first, and one may start moving riding it.

The end is not settled as one either. "What Is Creation" places the bundle as the completion in which things are integrated as a new form. pjdhiro sees the bundle as having both aspects: completion or deliverables, and, as a direction or gathering, supporting the next field. pjdhiro also sees that a collision may not reach binding: one side may win, or things may return to the field and wait for the next opposition.

Coming undone, drifting apart and going back are also included in development on this page.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):The image is like a depth-first search algorithm. I think there is something like exhaustive search that assumes misses. It isn't perfect. But it is reflected in later decisions. If you bundle it in language, it gets called "experience" or "failure". In the sense of a resource needed for development.

Depth-first search is a way of searching that goes down one path to a dead end, and if it is a dead end, goes back and searches the next path.

More — procedures, and the stance toward vision and planning

The same goes for the word procedure. pjdhiro includes in the definition of procedure what one does unconsciously. pjdhiro wants to include in the process what is tacit, what is irrational, and feelings and motivation, and to include in procedure even things that cannot be quantified, such as readiness of mind.

About vision and planning, pjdhiro holds the stance that vision and planning may be meaningless, may even be counterproductive in some cases, may be unnecessary in some cases, and necessary in others. pjdhiro even holds the viewpoint that vision and planning are only what human emotions want.

For this stance, pjdhiro named one word to apply.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):On this, I think the word taisho (dealing with things) might be good. For now.

Dealing with things is not directed only at what has already happened. pjdhiro sees envisioning as something done including not only what is happening but also what will likely happen and what may happen, and calls dealing with what has not yet happened dealing with uncertainty.

In the projects of life and family too

This is not limited to business. In the projects of life and family too, within one person, in a group of people and in society, processes not written in the sequence of vision, planning and execution are proceeding. pjdhiro's work over this year can also be read as one example. Within work begun as analysis, it was the one writing who changed (see also: "the app called me" in How I came to this question — background and history).

More — intended strategy, and strategy realized without being intended

The view that looks at both what is moved along intentionally and what takes shape without intention also exists in strategy research. In a 1983 chapter, Mintzberg and Waters gave four definitions of strategy. Two of them are strategy as a plan set out in advance, and strategy as a pattern seen in a stream of an organization's decisions and actions. Strategy as pattern, they write, is a definition concerned not with intention but with what was realized, that is, with what actually happened in the organization.

Combining these two definitions, they drew three strategies in one diagram: strategy intended and realized as intended; strategy intended but not realized; and strategy realized as a pattern without being particularly intended (emergent strategy).

What they followed were the strategies of organizations such as companies, newspapers, universities and government agencies. The range pjdhiro points to with the word development is not limited to organizations; it extends to change within one person, to families, and to the movements of society.

Sources: Mintzberg & Waters (1983) "The Mind of the Strategist(s)", in S. Srivastva and Associates, The Executive Mind, Jossey-Bass, pp.58–83 (full text checked against a scan of the book on the author's site; the four definitions p.59, the diagram and definitions of the three strategies p.60, the organizations studied p.61).

Then how does development relate to projects and creation?

On this page, the three can be read as names that touch one event from different sides. In the first question, projects are asked about alongside living, and include not only business but life and family. Creation is the shape in which things come into being, proceeding field → wave → edge → vortex → bundle. Development is that shape proceeding, driven by the driving source. Each name is right, and none is the whole, so this page does not fix the relation among the three as one.

More — the relation of the three as generally put, and how this page reads it

In Japanese dictionaries, a project is "a development plan for research, business and the like", and also "a plan; a planned undertaking; a research and development plan". In the field of project management, the outline is clearer. A public document of the Project Management Association of Japan, citing the definition in a 1985 glossary, writes of project work as non-routine, non-repetitive, one-time work to achieve a specific goal within certain limits of time, money and quality, and continues that, to complete that work as initially targeted, one plans, organizes, coordinates and controls. Creation, in the dictionary, is "making something new for the first time by one's own ideas or skills" and "God making the universe and all things" (all translated by Claude).

Generally, the three are placed as if nested. A project is the frame of a plan with a beginning, an end and a goal. Development is the act of putting something new to practical use within it, and creation is bringing something new into being for the first time. Each is spoken of as an act of an intending subject (a person, or God).

After reading this section, pjdhiro pointed out that the image of the word plan cannot be shared.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):Whether "a plan with a beginning, an end and a goal" refers to the ideal when tackling something for the first time, or to a routine one has done for years — I think the term plan has something psychological in it. Wanting to control the environment, wanting to put responsibility somewhere, wanting agreement. What everyone was faintly aware of, PMBOK 7 brought in line with reality as predictive and adaptive. There must have been people for whom that was inconvenient. To give an analogy, in the project called life, how does each of us think about the significance of making plans? Some of us think they're unreliable, and for others, wishing it would be so becomes energy.

On this page, people are both subjects of action and one vortex swirling within events. The divisions differ too. A project in general is one-time and aims at completion as initially targeted. In this page's table, neither the beginning nor the end of development is settled as one. In the five stages of "What Is Creation", the bundle becomes the next field again, and may return to the field without reaching integration. What Is Creation writes the five stages as the process by which beings come into being, not limited to human ideas or skills but including matter, phenomena and the mind.

Sources: (in Japanese) Seisenban Nihon kokugo daijiten and Dejitaru Daijisen, "プロジェクト (project)" and "創造 (creation)" (checked against the text on Kotobank, 2026-11-10) / Hiroyuki Hazama, "PM resource guide: project management" (Project Management Association of Japan, Rev.0, 11 March 2001; full text checked against the public PDF on the association's site; the passage citing the definition in the Engineering Advancement Association of Japan's Glossary of Engineering Project Management, 1985).

A name of the form "X-driven development" is usually the name of a development method, such as test-driven development. Then is Love-Driven Development also the name of a way of doing things?

The name Love-Driven Development was given afterward to events encountered again and again (background and history). The driving source is what I want to call Love, and people are inside it. On whether people place it first, pjdhiro says this.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):You can't consciously, actively place it first, but sometimes it has already been placed first unconsciously, and from others' point of view, that is the person's own doing.

Procedures are written on the premise that the person moving things can manipulate the other, so Love-Driven Development does not take the form of a procedure. Even so, people do not do nothing. Where not everything can be controlled, people deal with what is happening and with what may happen. What this page writes is the structure and process of that phenomenon. To this question, pjdhiro answers that Love-Driven Development is "both an analysis of the phenomenon and a way of dealing with it" (on the word dealing, see the fold "Procedures, and the stance toward vision and planning").

More — names of methods, and the name Love-Driven Development

Test-driven development is a way of working in which test code is written before the program itself, and the program code is written so as to pass those tests. A method is something people choose, use following a procedure, and use to come closer to the result they want. The "driven" in the name can be read as saying what the developer places first and proceeds from.

About this reading, pjdhiro says this of the "driven" in Love-Driven Development.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):The "driven" in Love-Driven Development does speak of what people place first. It's just that it can't be shown in symbols. It points to what lies ahead of people. All along. To the moon.

(See also: the finger pointing at the moon)

Sources: IT glossary e-Words, "Test-driven development" (in Japanese; checked against the text, 2026-11-10).

More — Wilber's integral theory, and the process of development

After reading this reader, pjdhiro named the philosopher Wilber's integral theory.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):I think Wilber's integral theory is close too. The process of development. I don't remember the driving source

In a 1997 paper, Wilber looked at consciousness and its development from four aspects: the inside of one person (intention), the outside of one person (behavior), the inside of a group (culture), and the outside of a group (society). Every aspect, Wilber writes, unfolds through a dozen or so stages. And he wrote that inner consciousness can continue a process of transcending while including the earlier stages.

In excerpts from a book that he later put on the author's site, the same view is written moment by moment. The whole of the previous moment is handed to the present moment as something given. It exists before the present moment registers it. But it does not stay merely given. The present moment takes it up, includes and transcends it, and reworks it.

Two places look close. One is pjdhiro's view in this chapter that "sometimes it has already been placed first unconsciously", alongside the point that the previous moment is given before one is aware of it. The other is that the inside and outside of one person and of a group unfold at the same time, which overlaps with "Who" in this chapter's table (one person, a group of people, or society).

Wilber also writes of something corresponding to the driving source. In the excerpts, he gives it the name Eros, and restates it as a "tilt" or a "pull" toward greater depth, complexity, consciousness and care. Alongside Eros, which reaches up, he also writes of Agape, which embraces what came before and reaches down. The way of speaking of a tilt or a pull seems close in the direction of its image to this reader's image of gravity (Isn't Love like gravity?). The pairing of Eros and Agape also sits next to the chapter on Peirce's agapasm.

There are also places that look different. Wilber gives Eros a direction toward greater depth, and lines up all the aspects and stages in one frame. Development in this chapter searches assuming misses, includes going back, and does not place its direction as one.

After reading this section, pjdhiro restated Eros and Agape in pjdhiro's own words.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):A feeling of seeking, and a feeling of embracing, right. A positive activeness, and an active passivity like acceptance. I treat both of those driving sources, while they stay vague, as what I want to call Love.

Wilber wrote what reaches up and what embraces what came before and reaches down separately, under the two names Eros and Agape. This reader does not separate the two, and keeps them under one name. If only the upward direction is seen as the driving source, the movement of the embracing side is placed outside the driving. If both are put into what I want to call Love, both seeking and moving forward and accepting can be read as movements within the same driving.

Sources: Wilber, K. (1997) "An Integral Theory of Consciousness", Journal of Consciousness Studies 4(1):71–92 (full text checked against the Wayback Machine record of the full text that was on the site of the journal's publisher, Imprint Academic; the four aspects and the stages in the abstract, the process of transcending and including in the section "The Further Reaches of Human Nature") / Wilber, K., excerpt A "An Integral Age at the Leading Edge" and excerpt B "The Many Ways We Touch" from Volume 2 of the Kosmos trilogy (full text checked against the Wayback Machine records of the excerpts the author put on "Ken Wilber Online", the author's site at the publisher Shambhala; the previous moment being given and reworked in excerpt A, part 4, the tilt of Eros in excerpt A, part 2, the pull in excerpt A, part 3, Eros and Agape in excerpt B, part 2).

How can the words of development be checked with this view?

Then how should the words used within development be checked?

In pjdhiro's view, vision, the words of the mind, and value are all names given to vortices born from the field, and vortices cannot be cut off from the field. It can be read that misunderstanding arises where they are cut off and handled like parts inside people or products. So in this part, we check the words used within development before applying theories or instructions. pjdhiro sees Love-Driven Development as something that arranges the words and numbers received before thinking and deciding (the input), a sieve that asks whether one is treating vortices as vortices. In this sieve, one first checks whether words such as value, trust, vision and creation are being treated as single things cut off from the field and the waves.

The three chapters that follow look at this in turn: when checking the words one has received (Where are the words of business misunderstood?), when giving something form (Where does design come in?), and what changes when one checks and how far it works (What changes when the input is arranged?). The first chapter deals with words used in business and management settings, so it limits the discussion to business. The other two take examples from business, but the discussion is not limited to business. What this view is good for and in what situations it is useful are looked at together in the Part 7 chapter "What is this view good for, and in what situations is it useful?".

Where are the words of business misunderstood?

The use of checking the words one has received is looked at with three words often used in business settings: vision, the words of the mind, and value creation.

Vision

Where does vision come from?

The order often shown as the way to run a business is to first research the market and draw up a vision, and then start moving. There is research that looked at how experienced entrepreneurs proceed. Dew, Sarasvathy and colleagues gave 27 entrepreneurs, who had founded several companies and had more than 15 years of experience, and 37 students at a business school (MBA), the same task of starting a business with a new product, and asked them to think aloud. Many of the entrepreneurs rejected methods of market research such as questionnaires. Those who said they did not believe the market forecast figures written in the task were 15 of the 27 entrepreneurs and 2 of the 37 students. One entrepreneur said that instead of researching, he would first go and sell, even before the machine existed.

The view this study rests on is called effectuation (it is also placed in the table of "What has management studies seen as the driving source?"). It is the view of starting from the means at hand and producing new ends. Dew and colleagues compare it to the difference between a cook who makes a dish by following a recipe and a cook who imagines what dishes can be made from the ingredients at hand, and see the thinking of experienced entrepreneurs as the latter form.

pjdhiro says something close to this form when introducing himself.

pjdhiro's experience (translated by Claude):When I introduce myself, I often say that I'm good at the kind of things a housekeeper does. I think there is an image of people who lead projects as strong people who have a vision of the future, have a conception and a plan for getting there, and have forecasts and convictions that pierce through uncertainty, and I'm not that kind of person, so please don't misunderstand. Against uncertainty, it may be a simple process of just doing (going to the site + judging and adapting) recursively.

A housekeeper puts together what can be done from the ingredients and tools in the house they visit and from how life is going there that day. This can be read as the work of the cook who imagines what dishes can be made from the ingredients at hand. The way of proceeding by repeatedly going to the site, judging and adapting also overlaps with what one entrepreneur in the study by Dew and colleagues said: instead of researching, first go and sell. It is one aspect: a vision may also take shape at the end of moving. About where vision comes from, pjdhiro also lines up other aspects.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):Vision may be something wonderful like wisdom, may be mere psychological defense, may be born at the end of action.

Where, why and how vision arises is still not well understood (the chapter "What drives a project?"). pjdhiro sees that until the driving source working before it, what I want to call Love, is understood, what vision is may not be understood either. If one fixes its source as one thing while the 5W1H, structure and process of what is taken as vision are not understood, one thinks one understands, and the searching stops. In the terms of the chapter "How do expectation and trust differ?", expectation rides on the image of the future that a vision holds. This may be where vision moves people. Whether that image foresees what lies ahead or is maintained to protect oneself from anxiety is hard to tell apart from the content of the image alone.

pjdhiro's view on the word business conception being called disappointing is in the chapter "How do expectation and trust differ?".

If we had called it understood with the atom

When the atom was found, the explanation that matter is made of atoms was established. But if we had called it understood there, the nucleus inside the atom, and the still smaller elementary particles, would not have been looked for.

More — is vision psychological defense? (an image carrying expectation, and clues for telling apart)

pjdhiro sees whether something ends in disappointment as depending on ability and on the strength of psychological defense mechanisms. Following this view, if ability catches up with the image, the image becomes reality. If defense is strong, it can be read that one either keeps the image without admitting the betrayal (idealization), or stops moving just before being betrayed.

That uncertainty about future threat makes preparation hard, and that this leads to anxiety (Grupe & Nitschke, 2013), was seen in the fold of the chapter "What is anxiety?". When one feels the unseen ahead as a threat and the signal of survival is welling up, a vision may be easily sought as a picture that fills what lies ahead. It looks like the same shape as the misapplication of trying to solve a problem of trust by achieving survival.

Clues for telling apart come from the difference between expectation and trust.

  • In pjdhiro's view, expectation is not something that sharpens estimates. If so, estimating is done with another tool. Kahneman and Tversky (1977) separated the view from the content of the plan at hand from the view from the distribution of outcomes of similar cases, and wrote that relying on the former tends to make plans optimistic. Whether the vision stays as it is when set beside the numbers of similar cases is one clue.
  • Whether the image is made only of good things, or includes what does not fit. In the terms of the chapter "When signals and thoughts part, one feels a lack", a vision that keeps what does not fit held as a kesson can be read as holding good and bad together. Whether it can be called trust is not decided here, since what trust is is still being searched for. Idealization keeps the image by splitting off what does not fit.

If expectation is the energy to start moving, then a vision working as defense may also keep moving people. Taylor and Brown (1988), summarizing research, argued that the tendency to see oneself and the future as better than they are is a feature of ordinary thinking, and seems to support other aspects of mental health. There is also the aspect that without expectation one could not have started. In the sequence of recovery from social withdrawal that pjdhiro cites (the chapter "How do expectation and trust differ?"), expectation and conceit are also placed first. The person telling them apart also stands in the same uncertainty. So telling apart is not done once; it is repeated while moving.

Sources: Kahneman & Tversky (1977) Intuitive Prediction: Biases and Corrective Procedures, Decision Research Technical Report PTR-1042-77-6 (a technical report commissioned by the US Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency; full text checked against the Wayback Machine record of the DTIC ADA047747 PDF (4 February 2015); p.2-2) / Taylor & Brown (1988) Psychological Bulletin 103(2):193 (full text checked against the PDF of the journal pages on the site of author Taylor's UCLA lab; abstract on p.193).

Sources

The study of the task given to entrepreneurs and MBA students: Dew, Read, Sarasvathy & Wiltbank (2009) "Effectual versus predictive logics in entrepreneurial decision-making: Differences between experts and novices", Journal of Business Venturing 24(4):287–309 (full text checked against the PDF of the journal pages placed on effectuation.org, the base of the authors' research; the 27 and 37 people in the abstract on p.287, how the entrepreneurs were chosen p.288, the rejection of market research and the numbers of those who did not believe the forecast figures p.298, the entrepreneur's words about first going to sell in Table 5 on p.298, calling the entrepreneurs' thinking effectual p.288, the logic of starting from means to produce new ends and the comparison with cooks p.293).

The words of the mind

Then where else, besides vision, can this view be used?

When Google's people operations department studied more than 180 teams inside the company, the people doing the analysis expected to find the combination of individual traits and skills that makes an excellent team. The result was different: how members interact with one another mattered more than who is on the team, and what stood out far above the rest was psychological safety. That term, psychological safety, is now sometimes received in workplaces as if it were a quality inside each individual. It can be read that a way of receiving that sees the goodness of a team as a combination of what lies inside each individual shows both in the researchers' initial expectation and in how the term came to be received once it spread.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):I feel that the misunderstandings likely to arise around management buzzwords about the mind could be explained by differences in viewpoint. I think that is a use of Love-Driven Development.

Words about the mind, such as psychological safety, GRIT, intrinsic motivation and negative capability, are often used in management settings. Many are written on the theory side as matters of groups and relationships, yet in the field they tend to be received as abilities, qualities or states inside the person, things to be cultivated, measured or drawn out. From the side this page has been looking from, reassurance, perseverance and motivation are all names given to vortices that field and fluctuation produce, with feet both inside people and between people. Each word works as it is in situations that suit it, and misunderstanding arises at differences in viewpoint.

More — word by word (definition, common misunderstandings, interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development, conditions under which it works)
Psychological safety
Definition A shared belief held by members of a team that the team is safe for interpersonal risk taking. It rests on trust and respect, and points to a climate in which people are comfortable being themselves (Edmondson, 1999). Schein writes of it as a condition that lowers learning anxiety so that disconfirming information can be received (Schein, 1995)
Common misunderstandings Getting along well; having no strictness. A system in which one is not punished. Something that can be made by declaring "please say anything" and putting systems in place. Something that is better the higher it is, in any organization
Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development The source encompasses both not having to fear punishment or ridicule, and being trusted and respected. In the field, only the former, which is easy to confirm with mechanisms and systems, is sometimes used. In the words of pjdhiro's question: hasn't it turned, before we knew it, into something with the nuance of caring only for lower-order needs?
Conditions under which it works pjdhiro sees it as perhaps working in work that deals with intangible things, such as services and design

Then, if one is psychologically safe and reassured, do good things happen?

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):No. Reassurance and safety sought in a spirit of self-protection or opportunism can also be a state like dependence through psychological defense mechanisms.

The reassurance Schein wrote of was a condition for being able to receive disconfirming information. When reassurance of the same name is sought as a condition for continuing to be protected from that information, it works on the side of keeping defense up rather than letting it down (see also: The mind has mechanisms to protect itself).

To read the theory of psychological safety, you can go from What Is Psychological Safety to the papers of Edmondson and Schein.

GRIT
Definition Passion and perseverance for long-term goals. Maintaining effort and interest over years despite failure, adversity and plateaus (Duckworth et al., 2007)
Common misunderstandings Endurance or guts. The strength to grit one's teeth and bear it. What people who quit did not have
Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development It can be read that one can keep persevering because one is drawn to a center and orbits around it. Measuring shows only the perseverance; what one was drawn by does not show up in the numbers
Conditions under which it works When describing, afterward, the behavior of people who keep going because they are drawn

In reading GRIT, what pjdhiro is concerned with first is what kind of endurance the perseverance at its core points to.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):GRIT is perseverance, isn't it. The thing called endurance hasn't been broken down much. I think it's close to wabi. Resignation and acceptance.

The definition above says maintaining effort and interest over years despite failure, adversity and plateaus. Within the same behavior of maintaining, different states of mind can be present. There is a hard endurance that pushes back with gritted teeth, and there is also a soft endurance that keeps going while accepting that things do not go as one wishes. Where it overlaps with the process of recovering after adversity (the resilience entry below) cannot be told from the definition either. If one measures perseverance and tries to cultivate it without separating what workings of the mind support that endurance, it may easily drift toward "endurance or guts" in the common misunderstandings row. The resignation and acceptance pjdhiro named in the card are the states of mind of the soft side of endurance.

pjdhiro describes that soft endurance with the image of wabi.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):For me, wabi has the image of endurance beyond resignation.

According to dictionaries and encyclopedias, the word wabi originally pointed to being dispirited and to worrying. In the life of medieval grass hermitages and the tea ceremony, it also came to be valued positively as a word for the quiet, lonely taste found in simplicity. The Edo-period tea book Zencharoku, quoted by the encyclopedia, writes that wabi is not giving rise to the thought of being constrained even when constrained, and not raising the thought of lack even when lacking (the fold below). This can be read as a form of endurance that, in a different direction from pushing back against lack and bearing it, receives the lack as it is and carries on. Bearing not knowing without hurrying to fill it is also treated in the chapter "What is anxiety?" and in the entry on negative capability below.

More — how wabi is written in dictionaries and encyclopedias

A Japanese dictionary treats wabi as the noun form of the verb wabiru and lists four senses. The first is feeling forlorn, worrying, being dispirited; the example given is a poem from the Man'yōshū. The second is enjoying a quiet retired life. The third is the quiet, tranquil taste spoken of in the tea ceremony and haikai: a calm, lonely feeling within simplicity. An example is attached from an early Edo collection of anecdotes, that Sen no Rikyū, as "the true meaning of wabi", always hummed the poem "To those who wait only for the flowers, I would show the spring of the grass between the snow in a mountain village" (translated by Claude). The fourth, written with the character 詫, is apologizing.

An encyclopedia takes wabi as the noun form of the verb wabu, and writes that it originally meant falling into misfortune and decline, being disappointed and dejected, and as a result feeling helpless and transient. It continues that in the thatched-hermitage life of medieval recluses this came to be highly valued as proof of freedom from a view that prizes worldly honor and interest, and further became the fundamental spirit of the "wabi-suki" tea of Takeno Jōō, Sen no Rikyū and others. The same entry quotes the following passage from the Edo-period tea book Zencharoku.

其不自由なるも，不自由なりとおもふ念を不生，不足も不足の念を起さず，不調も不調の念を抱かぬを侘なりと心得べきなり

It means: one should understand wabi as not giving rise to the thought of being constrained even when constrained, not raising the thought of lack even when lacking, and not harboring the thought of disorder even when things are not in order (translated by Claude).

Sources: (in Japanese) Seisenban Nihon kokugo daijiten, "わび【侘・詫】" (Shogakukan; full text checked against the public version on Kotobank) / Kaitei shinban Sekai daihyakka jiten, "侘" (Heibonsha; the section written by Nobuo Hori; full text checked against the public version on Kotobank; the passage from Zencharoku is as quoted in this entry).

Resilience
Definition A process in which positive adaptation occurs within significant adversity (Luthar et al., 2000)
Common misunderstandings An unbreakable mind. Individual strength acquired by training. What people who could not recover did not have
Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development In research, resilience is written as the flow of recovery that occurs after adversity, and occurs through both the person's own strength and the support around them. In workplaces, only the aspect of strength the person has tends to be taken out of it. In pjdhiro's view, recovery is close to a process of self-renewal in which a new self is created. Its key lies in self-acceptance of a kind that removes psychological defense mechanisms. Fluctuations and inner oppositions born in adversity swirl into a vortex, and from there the next self takes shape. The same shape as fluctuations opposing within one person and conflict producing something, seen in the value creation section of the chapter "Where are the words of business misunderstood?"
Conditions under which it works When looking, after adversity, at both the person and the field that supports them

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):…Perseverance is resignation and acceptance, and resilience too is, I think, a stage of development, or rather a state, produced by a state with few psychological defense mechanisms in the same way.

Intrinsic motivation
Definition Doing an activity for the inherent interest and satisfaction of the activity itself (Ryan and Deci, 2000). The theory depicts this, as a tendency to seek novelty and challenge, to extend one's capacities and to learn, as an expression of the positive potential of human nature
Common misunderstandings A psychological state that is in everyone and works the same way if drawn out. A good thing to be cultivated. Something raised by rewards and goals
Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development In pjdhiro's view, the misunderstanding lies in speaking of one aspect of an ideal state as a psychological state. pjdhiro sees the stages of adult development theory as the only thing that can express it well (the fold under the table)
Conditions under which it works Activities attractive for their novelty, challenge or beauty. There, choice and having one's feelings acknowledged strengthen motivation, while rewards contingent on performance, deadlines and imposed goals weaken it (Ryan and Deci, 2000)

Then where does intrinsic motivation well up from? The theory does not ask that; it treats it as a tendency acquired in evolution, and studies the conditions that draw it out and sustain it and the conditions that weaken it. This reader has looked one step further there. The view is that people, within the distortion of the field we want to call Love, feel in the body the gap between Love and me as a lack, and are moved by that lack (the chapter "That is the driving source of projects"). In this view, being moved by the interest of the activity itself is also a name given to one of the vortices that this driving produces. pjdhiro sees the same driving as drifting toward results, toward money, fame or power, or toward things that do not turn into money, such as knowledge (the viewpoint added on the entrepreneur's mind). The misunderstanding of intrinsic motivation can be read as lying in taking out from all of this only the ideal direction of growing and learning.

More — one aspect of an ideal state, and a psychological state

The "Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development" row of the table was written from the following words of pjdhiro's, said while talking about where intrinsic motivation is misunderstood.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):The misunderstanding of "intrinsic motivation" is that it speaks of one aspect of an "ideal state" — as a psychological state. But I think the only thing that can express that well is the stages of adult development theory.

Ryan and Deci (2000) depict intrinsic motivation as an expression of the positive potential of human nature, seeking novelty and challenge and wanting to learn. pjdhiro sees this as one aspect of a certain ideal state. In workplaces, it is received as a psychological state that is in everyone and works the same way if drawn out (the "Common misunderstandings" row of the table). What pjdhiro calls a misunderstanding is this mix-up.

What pjdhiro names as expressing the ideal state is the stages of adult development theory. From stage to stage, what looks most threatening and how one protects the mind against it change (the chapter "What looks threatening changes with the stage of development").

The theory also describes ways of moving that, even though driven from within, are not felt as part of oneself. Ryan and Deci called acting to avoid guilt or anxiety, or to attain ego enhancements such as pride, introjection. The mind's moves to protect itself from anxiety were seen in the chapter "The mind has mechanisms to protect itself". About psychological defense mechanisms and intrinsic motivation, pjdhiro says the following.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):People end up feeling along their psychological defense mechanisms, and even go as far as acting out. That can't be stopped by strong motivation. Like mental illness or personality disorders. So measures taken to "use intrinsic motivation in a convenient direction" are probably close to psychiatry or treatment

Sources: Ryan & Deci (2000) American Psychologist 55(1):68 (positive potential pp.69–70, introjection p.73; the full text was checked on the page "What is intrinsic motivation?" (in Japanese)). pjdhiro's two remarks are also placed on that page.

To read the theory of intrinsic motivation, you can go from What is intrinsic motivation? (in Japanese) to the paper of Ryan and Deci. The theory of stages can be followed from What are stages of mental development? (in Japanese).

Negative capability
Definition Being capable of being in uncertainties, mysteries and doubts without any irritable reaching after fact and reason. The poet Keats named it, in a letter to the poet's brothers in December 1817, as the quality that goes to form a man of achievement, especially in literature, and which Shakespeare possessed so enormously (Keats, 1817). In later research, for example, a paper studying public health nurses in Japanese local governments introduces it as the ability to stay in uncertainty and ambiguity without rushing to conclusions (Ujihara et al., 2026). 🚧 How the word has been used in psychoanalysis and clinical practice is under construction
Common misunderstandings It is taken as the endurance of gritting one's teeth and bearing what has no answer. It is also sometimes taken as indecision that puts off deciding, or as an individual ability to be trained and increased
Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development This passage of Keats's letter has no words of enduring or effort. The poet Coleridge, given as the opposite example, is written of as one who cannot remain content with half-knowledge. What anxiety is is looked at in the chapter "What is anxiety?" and on the separate page "What is anxiety?" (in Japanese). 🚧 How this word relates to the holding of Kesson-Driven Thinking and to pjdhiro's view in the GRIT entry (endurance as resignation and acceptance) is under construction
Conditions under which it works Keats closes this passage of the letter by saying that, pursued through, it would perhaps take one no further than this: that with a great poet the sense of Beauty overcomes every other consideration, or rather obliterates all consideration (Keats, 1817)

Conflict
Definition Psychology calls a state in which two or more motives or goals compete and one is torn over which to take conflict. Opposition between people over discrepant wishes, opinions or norms is also called interpersonal conflict (Saishin shinrigaku jiten). For ways of handling it, there is an arrangement that divides five modes — competing, collaborating, compromising, avoiding and accommodating — on the two axes of cooperativeness and assertiveness (Kilmann and Thomas, 1977)
Common misunderstandings Taking conflict as either good or bad. Either avoiding it or settling it quickly as something bad, or, conversely, seeking it as something that is better the more one clashes. Among researchers and practitioners too, an optimistic view that conflict raises performance and creativity and should be encouraged, and a pessimistic view that it usually harms functioning, have been argued side by side (Greer et al., 2012)
Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development pjdhiro sees conflict as having no good or bad: it may be bad, and it also has an aspect of collision just before creation. pjdhiro also sees much misunderstanding where an opposition of two things is sorted into good and bad by psychological impression (below the table)
Conditions under which it works The review sums up that conflict is less likely to harm functioning when conflicts over the content of the task can be handled apart from conflicts over personality and relationships, and when members feel they are within the same goals. Suppressed conflict grows over time and becomes harder to resolve (Greer et al., 2012)

Having read the conflict table, pjdhiro says the following about the good and bad of conflict.

pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):Conflict has no good or bad. While giving examples of bad cases, it also has a positive side, exactly as the collision just before creation. Like "a crisis is a chance!" With double binds and cognitive dissonance and so on, people judge an opposition of two things as good or bad by psychological impression, so I feel there is a lot of misunderstanding.

Research on team conflict does not settle conflict as one of good or bad either. According to the review, conflict over personality and relationships consistently tends to lower performance, and conflict over task procedures and roles tends to lower performance and satisfaction even more. For example, if an anesthesiologist speaks ill of a surgeon's character or competence in the middle of an operation, the tension produced only gets in the way of the work (the review's illustration). On the other hand, conflict over the content of the task showed, in a meta-analysis combining 116 studies, no relation to performance overall, and worked in the direction of raising performance in teams that could handle it apart from relationship conflict (Greer et al. 2012). "What Is Creation" writes the edge of the five stages of creation as "the border where heterogeneous things meet and collide".

More — how do conflict, cognitive dissonance and the double bind differ?

All three look like two things in opposition. What differs is where, and what collides with what.

  • Conflict: two or more motives or goals competing within one person. Lewin divided this into three types: wanting both (approach–approach), wanting to avoid both (avoidance–avoidance), and being both drawn to and wanting to avoid one goal (approach–avoidance). Discrepancies of wishes, opinions or norms between people are also called interpersonal conflict (Saishin shinrigaku jiten).
  • Cognitive dissonance: a state within one person in which things one knows or believes do not fit with one another. The misfit produces pressure to reduce it, and people may reduce it by changing their own thoughts or attitudes (Dejitaru Daijisen; Festinger and Carlsmith 1959). In Festinger and Carlsmith's experiment, people made to tell others that a boring task had been interesting, and paid one dollar, later themselves rated the task as interesting. Those paid twenty dollars did not change their ratings. It is written that they persuaded themselves to fit what they had said.
  • Double bind: 🚧 under construction (to be written after reading the original by Bateson et al. 1956).

Interpretation from the viewpoint of Love-Driven Development What moves that mind lies beyond the reach of the table. It connects to the hypothesis in "Is being itself the source?" in the chapter "What is still not known", that managers too may be one point orbiting a center
Conditions under which it works As a description of situations in which a vision brings people together
